**המכונות של ארכיטקטורה זו (במרשם: 14, מהן 11 התקנים, 10 בעלי שערים).** [**IonQ**](organisation/ionq.html) — Tempo (בהפעלה, 100), 40-ion Ba-133 qLDPC testbed (הודגם, 40), Aria (בהפעלה, 32), Forte / Forte Enterprise (בהפעלה, 30); **Alpine Quantum Technologies** — AQT LYNX (הודגם, 15), IBEX Q1 (Marmot / PINE) (בהפעלה, 12); [**Lebedev Physical Institute (FIAN) and the Russian Quantum Center, Rosatom roadmap**](organisation/russian-quantum-center.html) — 70-qubit ion quantum computer (FIAN / Rosatom roadmap) (הוכרז, 70); [**Poznan Supercomputing and Networking Center (PCSS)**](organisation/poznan-supercomputing-and-networking-center-pcss.html) — PIAST-Q (בהפעלה, 20); [**Quantum Art**](organisation/quantum-art.html) — Perspective (1,000-qubit multi-core system) (הוכרז); [**Qudoor (Guokaike)**](organisation/qudoor.html) — AbaQ (בהפעלה); [**Sandia National Laboratories**](organisation/sandia-national-laboratories.html) — QSCOUT (Quantum Scientific Computing Open User Testbed) (בהפעלה, 6); [**Tsinghua University (Duan group)**](organisation/tsinghua-university.html) — 2-D site-resolved ion simulator (הודגם, 512); [**University of Innsbruck**](organisation/university-of-innsbruck.html) — University of Innsbruck / IQOQI research trap (הודגם, 20); [**University of Maryland / Duke University**](organisation/duke-university.html) — Maryland / Duke Quantum Center (EURIQA lineage) (הודגם, 13).

*תאים מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה (9).* 40-ion Ba-133 qLDPC testbed: L8 `∅G-beam` (פער אטלס — beam-search decoder (Delfosse) for the qLDPC codes, run offline; no Atlas node for search ); Perspective (1,000-qubit multi-core system): L5 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; Perspective (1,000-qubit multi-core system): L9 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; AbaQ: L5 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; 2-D site-resolved ion simulator: L3 `∅G-isinganalog` (פער אטלס — global laser-driven spin–spin coupling; analog Ising simulator in a 2-D ion crystal, not a); 2-D site-resolved ion simulator: L4 `∅G-crystal` (פער אטלס — static 2-D Coulomb crystal, power-law all-to-all coupling (511 neighbours), no transport); University of Innsbruck / IQOQI research trap: L4 הסעת יונים (QCCD, צמתים, מלכודות רשת) (`cx_qccd`, חלופית שנלקחה מארכיטקטורה אחרת); University of Innsbruck / IQOQI research trap: L8 `∅G-coherent` (פער אטלס — measurement-free: syndromes mapped into an auxiliary register, coherent feedback unitary r); Maryland / Duke Quantum Center (EURIQA lineage): L7 `∅G-subsystem` (פער אטלס — Bacon–Shor [[9,1,3]] subsystem code on 13 ions: FT prep, FT measurement, logical rotation,).

#### 8.3.6 שערי יונים אלקטרוניים — יונים לכודים — בקרה אלקטרונית של הקיוביט (שערי מיקרוגל / RF) (`ion_elec`)

**The idea.** Take the laser out of the entangling gate: currents in the trap chip's electrodes make a microwave field whose gradient couples a hyperfine ion qubit to the shared motion (`g_elec`); drive traces are shared and local tuning electrodes select the ions [D][I42]. A microwave photon reaches no excited state, so laser gates' scattering floor vanishes: 8.4×10⁻⁵ on two ions, without ground-state cooling [D][I4]. Speed is the price, since a stronger gradient puts the ion nearer heating surfaces: 225.8 µs per gate [D][I4] against ~70 µs on Helios [D][I1]. The bet: control that scales like electronics, ~200 sources for 1,000 ions [S] (§7.5).

**What it needs.** The gate *requires* chip-integrated microwave control (`ct_ionmw`), which *requires* current traces in the trap: the chip is the control system, with no fallback to laser gates. Lasers remain for cooling, state preparation and fluorescence readout. Carrier, gate, control, readout and interconnect have one technology each; elsewhere the options *replace* each other: chain bus or shuttling, qLDPC or high-rate codes (both *require* all-to-all), Relay-BP or matching, trap microfabrication or a 300 mm CMOS line. Without transport the derived syndrome round is gate-limited: 1.52 ms, 1.4 ms of it in gates [S] (§7.4).

**Where it stands.** Six machines, two devices, no code, decoder or link run. QUARTET (Oxford Ionics/IonQ), at the UK's National Quantum Computing Centre, is the largest at 32 qubits (Table 8.3), a count no primary source gives; its evidence paper shows a seven-zone chip for up to 10 qubits [D][I42]. JION (eleQtron with Forschungszentrum Jülich), inaugurated on 3 Sep 2026, runs 30 ytterbium ions with no published fidelity [P][I43]. Hence no best error in Table 8.3: the 99.99% is a two-ion record [D][I4]. Superion 256 is orderable: SkyWater-made chips, ions trapped in prototypes, no 256-qubit fidelity [C][I30]; IonQ Superion 10K (commercial 2028) [R][I30] and NFQC-1k (≥1,000 physical, 50 logical qubits by 2030) [R][I34] are plans; XAPHIRO (QUDORA NFQC) is under construction — a first ion trapped in June 2025, fifty qubits due by January 2027; only its emulator is reachable.

**Exceptions and borrowings.** No cell leaves the architecture's slots; the exceptions are in the evidence. Superion 256 alone fills all ten layers, from plans: a qLDPC code from a resource estimate [S][I29], a claimed photonic link [C][I30], a Relay-BP cell reading "algorithm not named" while IonQ's published decoder runs on one CPU [S][I13] (mislabelled), a metastable-encoding alternate citing nothing. Layers 7–9 are IonQ's intentions. IonQ Superion 10K and NFQC-1k leave four layers undisclosed: early stage. JION's MAGIC (magnetic-gradient-induced coupling) addresses ions by frequency in a static gradient, a second mechanism inside a technology defined by the near-field one: drawn too widely. QUARTET's shuttling alternate has no timeline. Universal Quantum's iQPU (QCCD architecture) borrows the gate and control technologies and fits both architectures equally: a hybrid.

**The next test.** Superion 256 is due at NVIDIA's research centre and Florida International University in 2027 [C][I31][I32]. Confirm if it is commissioned by end-2027 near 10⁻⁴ two-qubit error over the full register, publishing gate time, heating and crosstalk (§5.2). Demote if it slips beyond 2027, or if its error lands tenfold above the two-ion record once hundreds of microwave channels share a die: electronic control would then remain the single-qubit layer of laser-entangled machines.

**What the builders say.** Oxford Ionics argues that one high-fidelity qubit is worth hundreds of poor ones, projecting 13 physical qubits per logical qubit and, without dates, 10,000+ qubits by replicating its 256-qubit design [C][I54]. IonQ, its owner since 2025, expects electronic control to cut the cost per qubit over 300-fold against lasers and takes orders for Superion 256, delivery in 2027 [C][I30]; with CMOS on the chip it promises full fault tolerance in its laboratory in 2027 and commercially in 2028 [R][I30]. eleQtron calls MAGIC, microwave control with a magnetic-field gradient, a clear path to scale [C][I23], and opened JION at Jülich in September 2026 [C][I55]. QUDORA's consortium aims within five years at a demonstrator of 1,000 physical and 50 logical qubits below 10⁻⁴ logical gate error [R][I56].

**המכונות של ארכיטקטורה זו (במרשם: 7, מהן 3 התקנים, 3 בעלי שערים).** [**IonQ**](organisation/ionq.html) — IonQ Superion 10K (מתוכנן, 10,000), Superion 256 (הוכרז, 256); [**Oxford Ionics (IonQ)**](organisation/ionq.html) — QUARTET (בהפעלה, 32); [**QUDORA Technologies**](organisation/qudora-technologies.html) — XAPHIRO (QUDORA 50-qubit NFQC prototype for the DLR Quantum Computing Initiative) (הוכרז); [**QUDORA Technologies-led consortium (TU Braunschweig, Leibniz University Hannover, PTB, Forschungszentrum Jülich, NXP Semiconductors Germany, AQT Germany)**](organisation/qudora-technologies.html) — NFQC-1k (מתוכנן, 1,000); [**eleQtron + Forschungszentrum Julich**](organisation/eleqtron.html) — JION (בהפעלה, 30); **eleQtron with NXP Semiconductors and ParityQC, for the DLR Quantum Computing Initiative** — QSea I (DLR QCI ion-trap demonstrator) (בהפעלה, 10).

*תאים מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה (10).* IonQ Superion 10K: L4 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; IonQ Superion 10K: L7 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; IonQ Superion 10K: L8 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; IonQ Superion 10K: L9 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; Superion 256: L8 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; XAPHIRO (QUDORA 50-qubit NFQC prototype for the DLR Quantum Computing Initiative): L4 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; NFQC-1k: L4 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; NFQC-1k: L7 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; NFQC-1k: L8 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; NFQC-1k: L9 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה.

#### 8.3.7 שערי רידברג אלקליים — מערך פינצטות רידברג — אלקליים (Rb/Cs) (`atom_rb`)

**The idea.** A rubidium or caesium atom in an optical tweezer holds a hyperfine clock qubit, coherent for 12.6 s in a 6,100-atom caesium array [D][N6]. A global laser pulse drives neighbours toward a Rydberg state, where one excitation blocks the other: a CZ gate in ~270 ns [D][N5]. Acousto-optic deflectors (AODs) move atoms or whole code blocks so any pair can meet: connectivity is a program. The price is the clock: transport and imaging, not the gate, set a derived QEC round of 1.31 ms [S] (§7.4), 1–4.5 ms measured [D][N5][N16]. The bet: qubits that cost laser power, not fabrication, and transversal logic between moved blocks, with constant syndrome rounds per logical gate, buying back the transport time [S][N17].

**What it needs.** The gate *requires* the alkali atom and global Rydberg pulses from free-space lasers (`ct_laser`). The high-rate and algorithmic-fault-tolerance codes *require* AOD transport: moving one block onto another pairs every qubit with its partner under one pulse. Algorithmic fault tolerance *requires* correlated, loss-aware decoding, which *requires* transversal circuits; its gain, 1.73(13)× over a loss-blind decoder, rests on four rounds of one run [D][N5], none in real time. Continuous reload *replaces* one-shot loading and *conflicts* with the stored qubits, which see scattered cooling light; a reservoir carried 0.5 m by conveyors mitigates it [D][N10]. Carrier, encoding, gate, interconnect and fabrication hold one technology each, code five, decoder four: the hardware is fixed, the code open. No atom–photon cavity link has joined two modules; AOD bandwidth runs out near 10⁴ atoms [S] (§2.4).

**Where it stands.** Thirteen machines, seven devices, five gate-capable. The largest, the 448-atom fault-tolerant processor (Harvard/MIT/QuEra), holds the best two-qubit error, 1.6×10⁻³ [D] (Table 8.3), and ran surface codes d=3→5 with 2.14× suppression over four rounds and up to 96 logical qubits [D][N5]. Gemini-class (QuEra; 99.2% global CZ, one shot per second [C][N4]) ran logical magic-state distillation [D][N12]; Sqale (Infleqtion) entangled 30 logical qubits in error-detecting blocks [C][N28]; Orion Alpha/Beta/Gamma (Pasqal) publishes no gate error. The largest arrays have no gate: the 6,100-qubit tweezer array [D][N6] and the continuously reloaded 3,000-atom machine, run over 2 h [D][N10]. Libra (QuEra, 2028: >256 logical qubits at 10⁻⁶) [R][N24] and Vela (Pasqal) are plans; Google's 2026 track names no species [P][N23]. Five rows have no documented cell: Hanyuan-1, Hanyuan-2, Qinghe-1, the Moscow State University prototype, the Quantum X Labs platform.

**Exceptions and borrowings.** The boundary with the analog simulator architecture (`atom_analog`) runs through layers 2 and 3: carrier, transport, control, readout and fabrication are shared, but the analog qubit is the ground–Rydberg pair and its computation continuous Hamiltonian evolution, with no code, decoder or link above. Pasqal crosses it both ways: Orion, filed here, lists the analog encoding and evolution as alternates; Fresnel, filed there, lists the hyperfine qubit and blockade CZ. One apparatus runs either mode, so the line divides modes, and a machine is filed by its primary one: hybrids, not errors. Orion's chip-optics alternate cites nothing. Gemini-class and Libra list fast readout as an alternate neither machine shows: mislabelled. Libra's ten-layer fit rests on a cavity cell specifying no link. The reload machine is dated August 2026 from a review; its paper is from 2025 [D][N10]. The Moscow prototype has no entangling gate yet a two-qubit error from a press statement; Quantum X Labs names no species; Hanyuan-2's inter-core link is undisclosed: data gaps. The alkaline-earth architecture shares gate, transport, reload, decoder and cavity; carrier and erasure encoding divide them.

**The next test.** Confirm by end-2027 (§5.2): a memory of ≥100 rounds with reloading at Λ>2 (error suppression per code-distance step) on an alkali array, and a gate-capable alkali processor above 1,180 qubits, the family frontier the alkaline-earth architecture has held since 2023 (§8.5, F1b). Demote if Libra slips past 2028, or two-qubit fidelity stalls at 99.9%, the ceiling a 2026 review sets for the standard CZ, with no new gate scheme [S][N7].

**What the builders say.** Harvard's Mikhail Lukin says the 448-atom processor combined, for the first time, all essential elements of scalable error-corrected computation in one architecture [D][N42]; Caltech says its 6,100-atom array has quantity and quality together [D][N6]. QuEra promises Libra on Amazon Braket in 2028, with over 256 logical qubits at a logical error of 10⁻⁶ [R][N24]. Infleqtion, after entangling 30 logical qubits in error-detecting blocks in September 2026, says it is on track for 100 in 2028 and 1,000 by 2030 [R][N27]. Pasqal's 2025 roadmap runs from 2 logical qubits in 2025 to 20 in 2027, 100 in 2029 and 200 in 2030 [R][N25]; in January 2026 it moved Vela, over 256 qubits, from 2027 to 2026 [R][N43].

**המכונות של ארכיטקטורה זו (במרשם: 15, מהן 7 התקנים, 5 בעלי שערים).** **Pasqal** — Pasqal Centaurus (מתוכנן, 500), Vela (הוכרז, 256), Orion Alpha / Beta ("Ruby") / Gamma (בהפעלה, 140), Pasqal Lyra (מתוכנן); [**QuEra**](organisation/quera-computing.html) — Libra (מתוכנן, 10,000), Gemini-class (בהפעלה, 260); [**Zhongke Kuyuan (Wuhan, CAS)**](organisation/zhongke-kuyuan.html) — Hanyuan-2 (הוכרז, 200), Hanyuan-1 (בהפעלה, 100); [**Caltech (Endres group)**](organisation/california-institute-of-technology.html) — 6,100-qubit tweezer array (הודגם, 6,100); [**Harvard / MIT**](organisation/harvard-university.html) — continuously reloaded 3,000-atom machine (הודגם, 3,000); [**Harvard / MIT / QuEra**](organisation/quera-computing.html) — 448-atom fault-tolerant processor (הודגם, 448); [**Infleqtion**](organisation/infleqtion.html) — Sqale (בהפעלה, 100); [**Lomonosov Moscow State University with the Russian Quantum Center**](organisation/moscow-state-university.html) — 72-qubit neutral-atom (Rb) quantum computer prototype (50 qubits in Dec 2024) (הוכרז, 72); [**Quantum X Labs Inc. (Nasdaq: QXL)**](organisation/quantum-x-labs.html) — Quantum X Labs neutral-atom platform (50+ qubits, claimed) (הוכרז, 50); [**Zhongqi Wuliang (Shanghai, CAS SIOM spin-off)**](organisation/zhongqi-wuliang.html) — Qinghe-1 (הוכרז).

*תאים מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה (8).* Hanyuan-2: L9 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; Pasqal Centaurus: L7 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; Pasqal Centaurus: L8 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; Pasqal Lyra: L7 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; Pasqal Lyra: L8 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; Orion Alpha / Beta ("Ruby") / Gamma: L2 קיוביט אנלוגי יסוד–רידברג (`enc_gr`, חלופית שנלקחה מארכיטקטורה אחרת); Orion Alpha / Beta ("Ruby") / Gamma: L3 אבולוציה אנלוגית תחת המילטוניאן רידברג (`g_rydanalog`, חלופית שנלקחה מארכיטקטורה אחרת); Libra: L9 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה.

#### 8.3.8 מערכים אלקליים-עפרוריים — מערך פינצטות רידברג — אלקליים-עפרוריים (Yb/Sr), מחיקה מובנית (`atom_ae`)

**The idea.** Ytterbium and strontium have a metastable ³P₀ level one clock-line photon above the ground state. Store the qubit there (the Metastable ('omg') erasure encoding on the Alkaline-earth atom (Yb/Sr) — erasure-native), and ground-state imaging light, dark to the qubit, reveals atoms that decayed out of it: errors arrive located, as erasures. The bet: a worse gate for a cheaper code. At 98% conversion the surface-code threshold rises from 0.94% to 4.15% [S][X8]; measured, a metastable CZ errs 0.016(1), 38(6)% flagged, against 0.0060 raw on the ground-state ¹⁷¹Yb qubit [D][N8][N2].

**What it needs.** A relay: the encoding *requires* the metastable manifold; the Mid-circuit erasure check *requires* the encoding, since only a qubit dark to imaging can be checked unread; Erasure-adapted codes *require* the flags and Correlated / loss-aware decoding, since a plain matcher cannot use them. Break a link and the machine is an alkali array with a heavier atom. Five layers have one option; *replaces* edges name the retreats: ground-state or optical encodings, surface code, millisecond imaging, one-shot loading. One *conflict*: reload light (MOT, repumper) depletes stored atoms; a repumper-free MOT and ³P₀ shelving, 13 s lifetime, mitigate it [P][N37].

**Where it stands.** Five machines, three devices (Table 8.3). Phoenix (Gen-1) and Gen-2, 1,180 ¹⁷¹Yb atoms (Oct 2023), is the largest gate-capable device, with the only two-qubit figure, 4.0×10⁻³ [C][N13]; its toric code ran 90 rounds, but suppression vanished with reloading included [D][N13]. Magne, 1,225 physical for ~50 logical, is being installed for early 2027 [R][N22][N29][N30]. The metastable Yb-171 erasure array (Princeton, 20 atoms) slowed [[4,2,2]] decay 1.9(4)× with unconditional erasure decoding [D][N8]. MAQCS and DiNAQC: a 1,000-qubit demonstrator at TRL 5 [C][N33]; the neutral-ytterbium QPU began hardware validation in August 2026 [C][N38]. 28 of 59 cells documented.

**Exceptions and borrowings.** All five fit here best; only Princeton runs the bet's encoding, check, code and decoder as primaries. Phoenix and Magne encode in the ¹⁷¹Yb ground-state nuclear spin (Yaqumo by inference); their erasures are imaged atom losses, as on rubidium [D][N5]. Phoenix's metastable-encoding cell is denied by its own note (mislabelled), its code is an Atlas gap, `∅G-toric` (code technology too narrow), its row joins ⁸⁷Sr and ¹⁷¹Yb generations. MAQCS encodes on the Sr clock line, 10–100 ms coherence [P][N26], in an optical lattice (`∅G-lattice`, shared with QUIONE), no code, an undisclosed core link; planqc's error-corrected line, LOGIQC, is ytterbium [C][N34]. Yaqumo's fast readout is inferred from 17.6 µs imaging of spinless ¹⁷⁴Yb [P][N39]: early stage. No machine has the Atom–photon cavity interface. Borrowers: OQC GENESIS (check), Superion 256 (encoding), IBEX Q1 (optical qubit), QUIONE (carrier). The architecture is drawn by species; erasure-native rests on one laboratory.

**The next test.** Consistent with §5.2: by mid-2027 Magne crosses the 1,180-qubit frontier (§8.5, F1b) and delivers 50 logical qubits, decoded with erasure flags on unconditioned data; loss detection alone confirms the carrier, not the encoding. By end-2027 a group outside Princeton and Caltech converts over 70% of a metastable CZ's error with residual below 3×10⁻³; if none passes 50% and the next Yb memory again loses suppression under reloading, the architecture demotes to a species choice within the alkali bet.

**What the builders say.** Atom Computing credits its ytterbium nuclear-spin qubits with record coherence, needed for deep algorithms [C][N44], and calls its June 2026 toric-code run practical error correction [C][N44]. Microsoft expects Magne, more than 1,200 physical qubits from Atom Computing, online in early 2027 [R][N45]. Princeton's argument for the metastable qubit is overhead: fewer errors, and the remaining ones shaped to be easier to correct [D][N8]. planqc plans MAQCS, its 1,000-qubit demonstrator, as a co-processor at LRZ by the end of 2027 [R][N33], and Yaqumo a ytterbium system of several hundred qubits with error correction by fiscal 2027 [R][N38].

**המכונות של ארכיטקטורה זו (במרשם: 6, מהן 3 התקנים, 3 בעלי שערים).** [**Atom Computing**](organisation/atom-computing.html) — Phoenix (Gen-1) and Gen-2 (בהפעלה, 1,180); [**Atom Computing + Microsoft, for QuNorth**](organisation/atom-computing.html) — Magne (הוכרז, 1,225); [**Princeton (Thompson group)**](organisation/princeton-university.html) — metastable Yb-171 erasure array (הודגם, 20); [**Yaqumo (+ Fujitsu)**](organisation/yaqumo.html) — neutral-ytterbium QPU (הודגם); [**planqc**](organisation/planqc.html) — MAQCS (מתוכנן, 1,000); **planqc, for the DLR Quantum Computing Initiative** — DiNAQC (מתוכנן, 100).

*תאים מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה (5).* Phoenix (Gen-1) and Gen-2: L7 `∅G-toric` (פער אטלס — toric code d=4 and d=6, up to 90 syndrome-extraction rounds; lower logical error at larger); DiNAQC: L7 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; DiNAQC: L8 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; MAQCS: L4 `∅G-lattice` (פער אטלס — no Atlas node: optical-lattice grid ('egg carton made of light'), no per-atom tweezer move); MAQCS: L9 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה.

#### 8.3.9 סימולטור אטומים אנלוגי — סימולטור אנלוגי של אטומים ניטרליים (מערכי רידברג, גזי סריג) (`atom_analog`)

**The idea.** Keep the alkali tweezer array with its lasers, transport and imaging; drop the gate: global Rydberg lasers drive the whole register under one Ising Hamiltonian, Analog Rydberg Hamiltonian evolution (`g_rydanalog`) on the Ground–Rydberg analog qubit (`enc_gr`). Waveforms Ω(t), Δ(t) and atom geometry are the program, a bit-string snapshot the output. The bet: for many-body physics (G1), native Hamiltonian evolution reaches hundreds of atoms now. It gives up memory, gates and any route to fault tolerance.

**What it needs.** The evolution *requires* the ground–Rydberg basis, a Rydberg level in the carrier and global Rydberg lasers; imaging, an imaging transition. Code, decoder and interconnect are empty by design (§7.6): here analog simulation stops being a quantum computer in the register's sense. The encoding is a basis, not a memory: |r⟩ cannot be shelved; Aquila's T2* is 5.8 µs, programs stop at 4 µs [C][N40]; errors integrate coherently with no instant for a syndrome, so no code attaches and no decoder has input. Without an entangling gate they are devices, not processors (Table 8.3 prints no gate-capable size, no two-qubit error). The one *replaces* edge swaps the species.

**Where it stands.** Three machines, three devices, none gate-capable. Aquila (QuEra, 256 Rb-87 atoms, on Amazon Braket since November 2022) [C][N40] carries the physics: (2+1)D string breaking in a gauge theory, no advantage claim [D][A6]. Fresnel / Fresnel 2 (Pasqal, deployed 2023–2024) publishes no error rates. QUIONE (ICFO, April 2024) images an ⁸⁴Sr quantum gas site by site in an 813.4 nm lattice, fidelity above 94% [D][N41]. §7.4's T₂ for the architecture is the ground–Rydberg qubit's own 11.4 µs (Aquila, spin echo) [C][N40] since 26 Sep 2026, no longer the carrier's 12.6-s caesium hyperfine record [N6], which a qubit with a Rydberg level cannot use. 25 of 33 cells documented. No machine postdates 2024; the vendors' later ones (Gemini-class, Libra, Orion, Vela) are gate-model.

**Exceptions and borrowings.** The dual-mode Pasqal machines mirror each other: Fresnel / Fresnel 2 runs analog as primary, its digital mode (Hyperfine / clock-state qubit, Rydberg-blockade CZ) and PIC-generated tweezers as alternates; Orion Alpha / Beta ("Ruby") / Gamma, on the alkali architecture, swaps the modes; all else matches. Each fits its own architecture seven technologies to five: the boundary with atom_rb is a mode switch in layers 2–3 of one hardware (hybrid). The gate separates simulator from processor; the line fault tolerance needs, a code, neither has crossed. QUIONE lacks encoding, gate and Rydberg state: a Bose–Hubbard gas in a lattice (`∅G-lattice`, shared with MAQCS) under a carrier named for erasure-native tweezer qubits, here by elimination (four technologies in slot): the architecture is too narrow for lattice quantum-gas simulators. Caltech's erasure-excising Sr Rydberg simulator [N9], which the new alkaline-earth edges describe, is not in the register.

**The next test.** Two expectations; §5.2 has no analog row. Confirmation: by 31 December 2027 a commercial analog machine publishes a many-body fidelity at 60 or more atoms or a T2* above 20 µs, or an analog result survives a year of classical challenge (§2.8). Demotion: if Vela and QuEra's next systems ship without analog specifications and no analog-first machine is announced by mid-2027, analog simulation is a mode, analog alternates of layers 2–3 on atom_rb, not an architecture.

**What the builders say.** Pasqal runs analog workloads today on hardware it designs to evolve toward fault tolerance [C][N31]. In June 2025 it promised a 250-qubit processor built for quantum advantage on an industry problem, to be demonstrated in the first part of 2026 [R][N25]. In September 2026 it reported simulating materials beyond the practical reach of classical computing [C][N31]: 256 atoms on Orion Beta reproduced the magnet TmMgGaO₄, checked against crystal measurements [P][A13]. QuEra says real-time gauge-theory dynamics with dozens of qubits on Aquila reach the limits of classical simulation [C][A14]. ICFO's case for QUIONE: quantum simulation can answer questions current computers cannot, such as why some materials conduct without loss at relatively high temperatures [C][A15].

**המכונות של ארכיטקטורה זו (במרשם: 3, מהן 3 התקנים, 0 בעלי שערים).** [**ICFO - Institut de Ciencies Fotoniques**](organisation/icfo-institut-de-ciencies-fotoniques.html) — QUIONE (הודגם); [**Pasqal**](organisation/pasqal.html) — Fresnel / Fresnel 2 (בהפעלה, 100); [**QuEra**](organisation/quera-computing.html) — Aquila (בהפעלה, 256).

*תאים מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה (4).* QUIONE: L4 `∅G-lattice` (פער אטלס — atoms held in a 2-D in-plane optical lattice plus light sheet at 813.4 nm; no per-atom mov); Fresnel / Fresnel 2: L2 קיוביט על-דק / קיוביט מצב-שעון (`enc_hf`, חלופית שנלקחה מארכיטקטורה אחרת); Fresnel / Fresnel 2: L3 CZ בחסימת רידברג (`g_ryd`, חלופית שנלקחה מארכיטקטורה אחרת); Fresnel / Fresnel 2: L5 פינצטות הנוצרות ב-PIC / אופטיקה משולבת לאטומים (`ct_pic_trap`, חלופית שנלקחה מארכיטקטורה אחרת).

#### 8.3.10 היתוך פוטונים — פוטוניקה מבוססת-היתוך (FBQC) (`ph_fusion`)

**The idea.** Single photons in path or time-bin encoding are never joined by a gate: Linear-optical fusion (`g_fusion`) measures two photons from small pre-made resource states in the Bell basis, destroying both, and Fusion-based fault tolerance reads the logical state from the pattern of outcomes. The fact exploited: a photon does not decohere, loss is its only error, and a failed fusion announces itself: an erasure at a known place. The price is determinism. The bet: a foundry can build fault tolerance if each photon loses under 2.7% end to end, the threshold with 6-ring resource states [S][P10].

**What it needs.** Fusion *requires* number-resolving detection to herald and switching with feed-forward to route the next photon within its flight time; the switches *require* the Photonic IC foundry, where each costs 0.1–0.2 dB of a ~0.5 dB per-photon budget [D][P1]. The code *requires* the Multi-photon resource-state factory (`src_resource`), an empty slot: fused states reach 8 photons at under 1 Hz against 24–168 photons at MHz [D] (§7.6). A 2026 re-analysis adds a logical-error floor set by fusion failure alone [S][P23]. Seven layers hold one technology each, and nothing inside the architecture *replaces* or *conflicts*; fusion itself forecloses the continuous-variable branch, *replacing* Gaussian gates as counting *replaces* homodyne detection.

**Where it stands.** Five machines, three devices (Table 8.3), no logical qubit. Omega chipset (PsiQuantum, February 2025), all ten technologies in slot, is a component set and is counted as such, not as a device: fusion Bell fidelity 99.22%, chip-to-chip Bell 99.72% over 42 m [D][P1]; its 7.8×10⁻³ is that single fusion, and its code and decoder cells are flagged no-QEC-cycle. Belenos and Lucy (Quandela, 12 qubits each; Lucy at CEA, 2025 [P7]) are the largest gate-capable devices; their 6.2×10⁻² is Ascella's 2023 figure [D][P24], and Lucy has 0 of 14 cells documented. The ETRI 8-photon silicon-photonic qubit chip publishes no error rate; Carina (QuiX Quantum, 8 qubits) awaits commissioning at DLR [P8].

**Exceptions and borrowings.** No cell falls off the architecture, yet it is drawn wider than its bet. Belenos, Lucy and the ETRI chip occupy the fusion technology with post-selected or probabilistic gates in a reconfigurable mesh, circuit-model linear optics that the register's own cells call "not FBQC fusion"; only Omega and Carina fuse. Quandela's fault-tolerance design is a hybrid of quantum emitters and linear-optical gates [P25]. Both Quandela machines carry an Atlas gap, `∅G-twofab`: a III-V quantum-dot source and a SiN/silica mesh, two fabrication technologies where the map allows one (layer 10 too narrow). Omega and Belenos list the resource-state factory, an empty slot, as an alternate technology; Belenos's own cell calls the route undemonstrated (mislabelled). The other four have no code, decoder or interconnect: early stage. On other architectures, Aurora, Jiuzhang 4.0 and Borealis use its detector technology.

**The next test.** By March 2028 (§5.2), a chain of three or more fusions, or a resource state beyond Bell pairs fused into a network, published with an end-to-end success rate, would show that the architecture's real clock, resource-state supply, exists. Demote if only single-fusion figures remain, fused states stay at eight photons, PsiQuantum's Stage C validation [P12] comes back negative, or its 2027 target moves.

**What the builders say.** PsiQuantum's co-founder, Jeremy O'Brien, argues that a useful quantum computer must fully leverage the semiconductor industry [C][P30]. In 2024 PsiQuantum planned its Brisbane site operational by the end of 2027, its first system near a million physical qubits [R][P31]; in June 2026, breaking ground at a new site in Moreton Bay, it set cryoplant delivery for the second half of 2027, with commissioning and chip cabinets to follow, naming no operating date [R][P32]. Quandela plans 100+ logical qubits in 2030–31 [R][P33] and says hundreds of logical qubits will draw under 500 kW [C][P33]. QuiX promised a universal machine in 2026 and error correction in 2027 [R][P34]; Carina's core hardware, designed for a universal gate set, reached DLR in July 2026 [C][P35].

**המכונות של ארכיטקטורה זו (במרשם: 6, מהן 3 התקנים, 3 בעלי שערים).** **Quandela** — Quandela Canopus (הוכרז, 24), Belenos (בהפעלה, 12), Lucy (בהפעלה, 12); [**ETRI (with KAIST and the University of Trento)**](organisation/etri.html) — ETRI 8-photon silicon-photonic qubit chip (הודגם, 8); [**PsiQuantum**](organisation/psiquantum.html) — Omega chipset (Moreton Bay / Chicago systems unnamed) (הודגם); [**QuiX Quantum**](organisation/quix-quantum.html) — Carina (הוכרז, 8).

*תאים מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה (3).* Belenos: L10 `∅G-twofab` (פער אטלס — needs two L10 nodes: SiN/silica interferometer meshes (fab_pic) plus GaAs quantum-dot sour); Quandela Canopus: L10 `∅G-twofab` (פער אטלס — quantum-dot source (III-V MBE) plus photonic-chip interferometer — two L10 nodes as for Be); Lucy: L10 `∅G-twofab` (פער אטלס — needs two L10 nodes: SiN/silica interferometer meshes (fab_pic) plus GaAs quantum-dot sour).

#### 8.3.11 GKP אופטי — פוטוניקה של משתנה רציף — GKP (`ph_cv`)

**The idea.** Squeezed light modes (`squeezed`), not single photons, carry the computation: beamsplitters entangle them deterministically into a time-multiplexed cluster state, CV Gaussian gates (`g_cv`) compute on it by homodyne measurement and feed-forward, and GKP grid encoding (`enc_gkp`) supplies both the qubit and the non-Gaussian resource that Gaussian operations lack. Xanadu's blueprint builds "three-dimensional hybrid resource states comprising both bosonic qubits and squeezed vacuum states" [S][P22]. Against fusion it trades probabilistic entangling for deterministic, and heralded erasures for analogue noise: loss acts as a random displacement that spends squeezing. The bet: photonic chips will make GKP states near the ~10 dB of squeezing the code needs before loss consumes them [S][P5].

**What it needs.** Gaussian gates *require* squeezed modes and room-temperature feed-forward; homodyne readout *requires* a local oscillator phase-locked by the same electronics, so detection, decision and modulation must close inside the 1 µs clock [D][P4]. Optical GKP *requires* that squeezing; the best on-chip GKP source reaches 0.62 dB effective [D][P5]. The code, bosonic concatenation with GKP under an outer qLDPC code, *requires* the GKP inner qubit, so it waits on that gap. Seven layers hold one technology each. The *replaces* edges fix the architecture's identity: Gaussian gates against fusion, homodyne against photon counting, Relay-BP against matching.

**Where it stands.** One machine, one device: Aurora (Xanadu, January 2025), 35 chips, 12 modes per 1 MHz cycle, a 12×N cluster state generated for two hours at ~14 dB total loss, no error-corrected computation [D][P4]. It runs the only in-loop decoder in photonics, within the 1 µs clock [D][P4], and 11 of its 12 cells are documented, the best-evidenced photonic machine. Table 8.3 still credits the architecture with no gate-capable device and no two-qubit error; the register flags Aurora detection-only and loss-dominated. Xanadu puts its loss 24× above threshold in 2026 [C][P9] and plans 1× in 2030, a fault-tolerance milestone in 2028–29 and 1,000+ logical qubits by 2031 [R][P9]; it holds a DARPA QBI Stage B slot [S28].

**Exceptions and borrowings.** An architecture of one machine cannot show whether it is drawn well; it is drawn around one design. Aurora fits every slot, with no gap and no none, but its alternates amend the edges. Number-resolving detection heralds its GKP states, so the architecture needs both detector technologies its *replaces* edge treats as rivals: counting to make the qubit, homodyne to compute. Its decoder is matching, with Relay-BP, the architecture's primary for the qLDPC outer code, as alternate. The gate alternate, fusion, has no machine. Jiuzhang 4.0 and Borealis, Aurora's predecessor, share the squeezed carrier and hold the Gaussian-gate technology as an off-architecture alternate without feed-forward: a Gaussian sampler is this architecture minus feed-forward and GKP states. Encoding and code are shared with the microwave cat-and-GKP architecture.

**The next test.** By March 2028 (§5.2): an on-chip optical GKP state above 1 dB effective squeezing, and a published loss factor within 10× of threshold; Xanadu's own schedule, 24× in 2026 to 1× in 2030, needs a gain of ~2.2× a year [S]. Both would keep the 2028–29 milestone credible. Demote if effective squeezing stays below 1 dB through 2027, or the milestone moves.

**What the builders say.** Xanadu chose GKP qubits because their logic runs by deterministic, room-temperature techniques and the states travel between chips over standard fibre [C][P36]. It says Aurora could in principle grow to thousands of server racks and millions of qubits [C][P37], and names optical loss as the next hurdle [C][P36]. Its August 2026 roadmap cuts a 'key loss technical indicator', which it does not define, from 24.1× in 2026 to 1.0× in 2030 [R][P9]; its chief executive expects fault tolerance in 2028–29 [R][P9]; and it promises up to 200 logical qubits by 2029, 500 by 2030 and over 1,000 by 2031 [R][P9].

**המכונות של ארכיטקטורה זו (במרשם: 1, מהן 1 התקנים, 0 בעלי שערים).** [**Xanadu**](organisation/xanadu.html) — Aurora (הודגם, 12).

*תאים מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה:* אין — כל תא של כל מכונה נמצא במשבצת של הארכיטקטורה.

#### 8.3.12 דגימת בוזונים — דוגם בוזונים (`ph_sampler`)

**The idea.** Squeezed light or single photons enter a Programmable linear-optical interferometer (`g_lointer`) and are counted at its outputs; the output probabilities are hafnians of matrices built from the network (permanents for single photons), #P-hard to compute [G][P28]. No entangling gate, no feed-forward, no qubit: the only claim is that no classical computer reproduces the samples (goal G1). Where the fusion architecture builds qubits from small resource states and the continuous-variable architecture encodes them in GKP grids, this architecture skips the qubit. The bet: at high photon number the statistics outrun classical samplers before loss hands the output back to them.

**What it needs.** The interferometer *requires* light in the mesh and room-temperature electro-optic phase shifters; Bulk-optics and fibre assembly (`fab_bulk`) *requires* fibre-coupled modulators and delay loops. The carriers *replace* each other, as do photon counting and homodyne detection, and bulk assembly and the photonic IC foundry. Everything else is transmission: noisy Gaussian boson sampling becomes efficiently simulable once squeezing, transmission and detector efficiency fall below a bound [S][P29]. Code, decoder and interconnect are ∅ by design (§7.3): a sample has no logical state to protect, so loss is modelled rather than corrected, and classical challengers exploit it [P6].

**Where it stands.** Three machines, three devices, none gate-capable (Table 8.3). Jiuzhang 4.0 (USTC, May 2026): 1,024 squeezed states in 8,176 modes, up to 3,050 detected photons, 51% system efficiency, one sample per 25.6 µs [D][P6]. Borealis (Xanadu, 2022): 216 squeezed modes through three fibre loops, TES number-resolving detectors, up to 219 photons, one sample per 36 µs [D][P27]; the register flags its claim eroded. PT-2 (ORCA, deployed 2024–26) samples photon-subtracted squeezed states in a two-loop time-bin interferometer with a number-resolving detector [P][P26]; ORCA publishes no mode count, clock or loss. For the register the ∅ layers mean three things: no sampler enters the gate, code or decoder counts; their nine none cells in layers 7–9 are the architecture, not a delay, and inflate Table 8.2's maturity profile; three of photonics' eight devices (Table 8.1) are samplers, so the family's device count overstates its processors.

**Exceptions and borrowings.** Jiuzhang 4.0 and Borealis hold CV Gaussian gates, the continuous-variable architecture's technology, as an off-slot layer-3 alternate: a Gaussian interferometer without homodyne feed-forward. That is lineage, not mislabelling; Borealis's successor is Aurora. Both leave layer 2 none. The encoding technology, Time-bin / path photonic encoding, rests on PT-2 alone and fits badly: PT-2 fills every time bin, nominally one photon each [P][P26], so its bins are modes, not the two-bin qubit the technology defines; and the technology *requires* a single-photon carrier, while PT-2's is squeezed light. No machine uses the single-photon or homodyne alternates either: in layers 1, 2 and 6 the architecture is drawn wider than its register.

**The next test.** By 31 December 2027: no published classical sampler matches Jiuzhang 4.0 on its own benchmarks, and ORCA publishes PT-3's modes, loops and loss with a classical comparison. Demote if a classical algorithm matches Jiuzhang 4.0, as one matched each earlier advantage claim, or if PT-3, due in 2026 and undelivered (§2.5), ships without those figures.

**What the builders say.** USTC presents Jiuzhang 4.0 as more than a sampler: Gaussian boson sampling can also generate bosonic error-correcting codes [D][P6], and control of thousands of photons, it says, reaches far beyond classical tractability and opens a path to fault-tolerant photonic hardware [D][P6]. ORCA's case is time multiplexing: fibre delays let each photon source be reused many times [C][P38]. In May 2025 its chief executive expected quantum advantage within 24 months, from PT-3, then due in early 2026 [R][P39]; in June 2026 ORCA installed a PT-2 at a Japanese enterprise and scheduled its upgrade to PT-3 for later in the year [R][P40].

**המכונות של ארכיטקטורה זו (במרשם: 7, מהן 7 התקנים, 0 בעלי שערים).** **USTC (Pan Jianwei group)** — Jiuzhang (1.0) (הוצא משימוש), Jiuzhang 2.0 (הוצא משימוש), Jiuzhang 3.0 (הוצא משימוש), Jiuzhang 4.0 (הודגם); **ORCA Computing** — PT-1 (בהפעלה), PT-2 (בהפעלה); [**Xanadu**](organisation/xanadu.html) — Borealis (בהפעלה).

*תאים מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה (2).* Jiuzhang 4.0: L3 שערים גאוסיים CV + פעולות לא-גאוסיות בסיוע GKP (`g_cv`, חלופית שנלקחה מארכיטקטורה אחרת); Borealis: L3 שערים גאוסיים CV + פעולות לא-גאוסיות בסיוע GKP (`g_cv`, חלופית שנלקחה מארכיטקטורה אחרת).

#### 8.3.13 קיוביטי ספין ב-CMOS — ספינים בנקודות קוונטיות בסיליקון / גרמניום (`spin_qd`)

**The idea.** A Gate-defined quantum-dot spin (`qd_spin`) is a transistor-sized gate stack: printed by a 300 mm CMOS foundry (`fab_cmos`), entangled by a pulse on the barrier to its neighbour, the Exchange gate (`g_exch`), read by Spin-to-charge conversion (`ro_s2c`), and coherent 1.31 ms (Hahn echo) on foundry ²⁸Si [D][Q7]. The bet: dots uniform enough to share calibration, with control moved into the cryostat, scale like transistors. The price: a readout-limited clock, 8.5 µs derived [S] against ~100–300 µs as run [G] (§7.4), and reach only to neighbours unless spins move.

**What it needs.** Exchange *requires* the dots and baseband pulses: its strength depends exponentially on barrier voltage, and HRL puts ~80% of its CNOT error on control and calibration [D][Q2]. Carrier, cryo-CMOS, conveyor and the Cryogenic signal fan-out all *require* the foundry; the fan-out also *requires* cryo-CMOS behind it: Intel's Pando Tree serves up to 64 qubit terminals at 10–20 mK from one signal and nine digital lines of Horse Ridge II, 0.156 lines per terminal [C][Q30]. The surface code *requires* a 2-D nearest-neighbour lattice, which no register device shows; qLDPC *conflicts* with it (long-range checks), needing the conveyor that *replaces* it. Crossbar lines *conflict* with per-pair calibration. Six layers hold one technology; none links modules.

**Where it stands.** Nine machines, five devices (Table 8.3): demonstrated, the HRL exchange-only silicon spin processor (18 EO qubits from 54 quantum dots — the largest gate-capable device, added to the register on 26 Sep 2026 [D][Q2]), Tunnel Falls (Intel; 12 qubits [D][Q9]) and the foundry 8-qubit array (Diraq/imec, exchange in one of four pairs [D][Q7]); deployed, Bell-1 (UnityQ) (Equal1) and QM-One (Quantum Motion, count unpublished [Q10]); announced, the Diraq 8-qubit silicon spin quantum computer at Equinix Sydney, Groove Quantum and the Hitachi x Intel 18A spin programme; planned, Alloy Pioneer (Quobly), cloud access by end-2026 [R][Q16]. The best two-qubit error among devices, 3.0×10⁻³, is HRL's [D][Q2]; Equal1's product page claims 99.3% (7.0×10⁻³) [C][Q31] where a press survey prints 98.4% [P][Q32]; no other machine publishes one. Off-register bests: 99.04–99.56% on foundry two-qubit devices [D][Q1]. HRL's bit-flip repetition code, error falling 4.7× from distance 3 to 5, sequenced from 4 K, is the architecture's only error-correction run [D][Q2].

**Exceptions and borrowings.** No off-slot cell or better-fitting architecture. Until 26 Sep 2026 the primaries described an absent machine — all eight rows used Single-spin (Loss–DiVincenzo) encoding, none Exchange-only encoding; HRL's device, now IBM's [Q18], is now the ninth row and the architecture's only carrier of its primary encoding, with its repetition code and parity decoder as Atlas gaps (`∅G-repetition`, `∅G-parity`). The foundry 8-qubit array holds the only code cell, a roadmap qLDPC with shuttling as alternate (early stage), and carries 1.1×10⁻² from other devices at 1 K [D][Q5]. Bell-1's on-die control is an Atlas gap (`∅G-qsoc`), QM-One's is not. Tunnel Falls mixes cryo-CMOS and room-temperature drive (hybrid); Equinix control is undisclosed. Only Tunnel Falls documents its fan-out; three room-temperature machines should read none. Five drive single spins by ESR or EDSR, a technology only the colour-centre architecture holds: layer 3 is too narrow. Borrowers: cryo-CMOS on five superconducting machines; the foundry on thirteen, eight ion traps.

**The next test.** By March 2028 (§5.2): a 300 mm array above 20 qubits with all-pairs two-qubit fidelity ≥ 99.5% and µs-class readout confirms; Diraq's 2029 target (150 k physical, 1 k logical [R][Q14]) cut again demotes. If the Equinix system (due October 2026 [C][Q20]) and Alloy Pioneer reach 2028 without their own two-qubit error, the best number stays a vendor's.

**What the builders say.** Diraq argues from the foundry: its qubits are made like the chips in phones and laptops, a path to millions of qubits on one chip [C][Q33]. Quobly, making its chips on STMicroelectronics' 300 mm line, targets one million qubits by 2032, after cloud access to its first machine, Alloy Pioneer, by end-2026 [R][Q16]. Hitachi, with Intel and AIST, plans error-corrected prototypes of 100 qubits in fiscal 2028 and 1,000 in fiscal 2030 on Intel's 18A process [R][Q34]. HRL answers the wiring problem with a cryogenic CMOS controller that ran error correction on 18-qubit devices without room-temperature control [D][Q2]. Equal1 fits a silicon-spin computer cooled to 0.3 K into one 19-inch rack [C][Q23]; Quantum Motion's QM-One takes three [C][Q10].

**המכונות של ארכיטקטורה זו (במרשם: 10, מהן 7 התקנים, 7 בעלי שערים).** [**Equal1**](organisation/equal1.html) — Bell-1 (UnityQ) (בהפעלה, 6), RacQ (בהפעלה); [**Diraq**](organisation/diraq.html) — foundry 8-qubit array (הודגם, 8); [**Diraq (with Equinix)**](organisation/diraq.html) — Diraq 8-qubit silicon spin quantum computer at Equinix Sydney (הוכרז, 8); [**Groove Quantum**](organisation/groove-quantum.html) — Groove Quantum 18-qubit germanium spin processor (הודגם, 18); [**HRL Laboratories (IBM since 26 Aug 2026)**](organisation/hrl-laboratories-ibm.html) — HRL exchange-only silicon spin processor (18 EO qubits from 54 quantum dots) (הודגם, 18); [**Hitachi + Intel**](organisation/hitachi.html) — Hitachi x Intel 18A spin programme (הוכרז, 100); [**Intel**](organisation/intel.html) — Tunnel Falls (הודגם, 12); [**Quantum Motion**](organisation/quantum-motion.html) — QM-One (בהפעלה); [**Quobly**](organisation/quobly.html) — Alloy Pioneer (Quobly) (מתוכנן, 15).

*תאים מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה (8).* Diraq 8-qubit silicon spin quantum computer at Equinix Sydney: L5 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; Bell-1 (UnityQ): L5 `∅G-qsoc` (פער אטלס — Quantum System on Chip: qubits and control/readout electronics on the same 22FDX die at 0.); RacQ: L5 `∅G-qsoc` (פער אטלס — quantum system-on-chip: qubits, control electronics and error-correction logic on one CMOS); RacQ: L8 מפענח קריוגני / על השבב (CMOS קריוגני, SFQ) (`dec_cryo`, חלופית שנלקחה מארכיטקטורה אחרת); HRL exchange-only silicon spin processor (18 EO qubits from 54 quantum dots): L7 קודים לגילוי שגיאות ([[4,2,2]], קוד משטח d = 2, מרחב-זמן) (`code_detect`, ראשית מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה); HRL exchange-only silicon spin processor (18 EO qubits from 54 quantum dots): L7 `∅G-repetition` (פער אטלס — d=5 bit-flip repetition code on 7 exchange-only qubits, Λ5/3 = 4.7 over up to 200 rounds, ); HRL exchange-only silicon spin processor (18 EO qubits from 54 quantum dots): L8 `∅G-parity` (פער אטלס — repetition-code syndromes interpreted after the run with a parity-check (majority) decoder); QM-One: L5 `∅G-qsoc` (פער אטלס — cryo-CMOS integrated on the qubit die).

#### 8.3.14 ספינים דונוריים — ספינים דונוריים בסיליקון (`spin_donor`)

**The idea.** A Donor spin (P in ²⁸Si) (`donor`), written atom by atom by STM hydrogen lithography (`fab_stm`): the ³¹P nucleus is the data qubit, the bound electron its drive, coupler and readout probe. The fact exploited: a nucleus has no orbital degree of freedom, hence no first-order coupling to the charge noise that holds foundry dots at 99.04–99.56% [D][Q1]; every gate still passes through the electron, which has one. The bet (Table 8.3): the longest coherence in a solid is worth a write process with no foundry.

**What it needs.** Ten layers, one technology each, no alternates. The donor *requires* STM placement because exchange between donor electrons falls exponentially with separation, so a donor a few nanometres off leaves a dead register [G]. Exchange *requires* baseband gate pulses; readout *requires* a charge sensor beside the electron; the surface code *requires* a 2-D lattice, which two registers of four and five nuclei [D][Q3] do not form. Two *replaces* edges, dot vs donor and foundry vs STM, separate it from the quantum-dot architecture; the other eight technologies are shared. The borrowed fan-out *requires* a CMOS router die and cryo-CMOS control, both absent here: a large array would be a hybrid with the dot architecture.

**Where it stands.** One machine, one device: the 11-qubit donor processor (Silicon Quantum Computing), demonstrated: nine ³¹P nuclear qubits in two registers with two electron ancillas; an electron CROT at 99.64% in 1.25 µs, 3.6×10⁻³, the family's best among devices (§8.4, H2); single-qubit gates to 99.99%; GHZ states over eight nuclei [D][Q3]. Nuclear Hahn echo reaches 660 ms [P][Q27]. No code runs; readout is non-demolition through the electron; control sits at room temperature. Six of ten cells documented. SQC holds a QBI Stage B slot [S28]; its roadmap dates no intermediate milestone. Flag: no foundry route.

**Exceptions and borrowings.** A single machine cannot test the architecture's drawing; SQC, fitting no other architecture better, amends layers 3 to 5. Inside a register the nuclei couple all-to-all through the shared electron, a star rather than a lattice; nearest neighbour holds only between registers (layer 4 too narrow). The nuclear gates are hyperfine-conditional rotations driven by ESR and NMR, not exchange, which serves only the electron CROT; baseband control excludes that resonant drive: the colour-centre architecture's Microwave / optical spin gates (`g_mwspin`) fit better (layers 3 and 5 too narrow). The fan-out cell records the missing slot, not a component (data gap: none). The manufacturing cell's SkyWater partnership is no foundry route: SkyWater's stated role is superconducting resonators and packaging [C][Q28] and it has been IonQ's since 31 July 2026 [C][I21]. No other architecture uses donor or STM; single-spin encoding and baseband control recur on colour-centre machines and on Majorana 1 (and Majorana 2).

**The next test.** By March 2028 (§5.2 names no donor milestone): a donor device beyond eleven qubits, or a placement-yield distribution for SQC's automated patterning, hours to minutes per 70-dot device and hundreds of thousands of dots patterned [C][Q29], confirms the write step scales; eleven qubits and no yield figure then demote it to a physics testbed. QBI Stage C would be the first independent check.

**What the builders say.** Silicon Quantum Computing argues from purity and precision: its processors contain only silicon and phosphorus atoms, which it says cuts bit-flip errors [C][Q35], and it places phosphorus atoms with 0.13 nm precision and claims qubit quality rises as qubits are added [C][Q36]. Its 11-qubit processor, two multi-atom registers, reached Bell-state fidelities up to 99.5% on local and non-local nuclear-spin pairs [D][Q3]. In November 2025 SQC entered Stage B of DARPA's Quantum Benchmarking Initiative, committing to a detailed research roadmap to 2033 [C][Q37], the year its site gives for universal, fault-tolerant quantum computers [R][Q35].

**המכונות של ארכיטקטורה זו (במרשם: 1, מהן 1 התקנים, 1 בעלי שערים).** [**Silicon Quantum Computing**](organisation/silicon-quantum-computing.html) — 11-qubit donor processor (הודגם, 11).

*תאים מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה:* אין — כל תא של כל מכונה נמצא במשבצת של הארכיטקטורה.

#### 8.3.15 צומתי ספין–פוטון — ספינים במרכזי צבע — צומתי רשת (NV/SiV/T) (`defect`)

**The idea.** A point defect in diamond (NV, SiV, SnV) or silicon (T centre) (`defect`) has a spin-selective optical transition: its electron spin emits a photon entangled with it, and nearby nuclear spins under hyperfine-conditional microwave gates (`g_mwspin`) keep a small memory. Heralded photons (`ic_spinphoton`), not on-chip wiring, join these few-qubit nodes. Table 8.3's bet fuses two on one carrier: a room-temperature NV accelerator and cryogenic network nodes. Both forgo a dense lattice; for the nodes, link rate, not gate error, is the clock.

**What it needs.** The own technologies *require* the carrier — gates its spin register, fluorescence readout its optical transition, the link its spin–photon interface — and the carrier *requires* a host crystal, whose only technology is diamond (`fab_diamond`). Five technologies are borrowed: encoding, readout, baseband control, nearest-neighbour lattice, qLDPC code. Two clash: the code *conflicts* with the lattice, needing degree-6 checks that here only the optical link supplies. No decoder (§7.6): a code named, none decoded.

**Where it stands.** Three machines, all devices, no two-qubit error (Table 8.3). The largest, Quoll / QB-QDK 2.0 (Quantum Brilliance), is deployed: 6 qubits in the register, room-temperature units at Oak Ridge, no gate fidelity [C][Q24]. Entanglement First modules (Photonic Inc, QBI Stage B [S28]) teleported a state into a remote matter node over 30 km of commercial fibre (February 2026), no rate or fidelity [P][Q25]. Fujitsu's Diamond-spin quantum computer prototype carries SnV centres on photonic circuits at −271.6 °C, no qubit count or fidelity, multi-module by 2027 [C][R][Q21]. Per-node figures: NV gate errors below 0.1% [P], SiV entanglement over 35 km at ~1 Hz [D], a post-selected T-centre teleported CNOT [P] [Q13].

**Exceptions and borrowings.** All three fit here best; none fits whole. Photonic connects any-to-any through the optical link (`∅G-optlink`), which its SHYPS qLDPC code needs, claimed at 20× fewer physical qubits per logical [C][Q26]; its silicon host has no technology (`∅G-sicolour`): layers 4 and 10 are drawn too narrowly. Quoll lacks link, code and decoder, and its microwave-and-laser control has no technology (`∅G-nvroom`): a processor on a network architecture, drawn too widely. Fujitsu's prototype adds a photonic-IC foundry (`fab_pic`), states no connectivity and shares the control gap at 1.55 K: an early hybrid. Only Photonic uses the namesake link; no other architecture borrows the architecture's own technologies.

**The next test.** By the technology briefs (§5.2 has no defect row), confirmation by March 2028 is an unconditional inter-node gate above 90%, a heralded link above 1 kHz, or link rate and fidelity from Fujitsu's multi-module prototype. Demotion: Photonic's links still without rate and fidelity, and the SnV modules without a qubit count, at end-2027; then the network bet is repeater and sensing technology; Quoll needs its own architecture.

**What the builders say.** Fujitsu, with TU Delft and QuTech, built a working diamond-spin prototype with tin-vacancy centres on photonic chips [C][Q21], chose the route for its optical links between modules and plans a multi-module prototype by 2027 [R][Q21]. Photonic argues that T-centre spin qubits in silicon, linked by light, give high connectivity, efficient error correction and a fit with telecom networks [C][Q38]; in 2024 it ran a teleported CNOT between qubits in different cryostats [C][Q39]. Quantum Brilliance bets on nitrogen-vacancy qubits at room temperature and pressure, which its CEO says decides where the machines can go and who can use them [C][Q40]; Fraunhofer IAF bought its rack-mounted QB-QDK2.0 [C][Q41].

**המכונות של ארכיטקטורה זו (במרשם: 5, מהן 5 התקנים, 5 בעלי שערים).** **SaxonQ** — QC2026 Dual Core (הודגם, 10), SXQ4 (בהפעלה, 4); [**Fujitsu (with TU Delft and QuTech)**](organisation/fujitsu.html) — Diamond-spin quantum computer prototype (הודגם); [**Photonic Inc.**](organisation/photonic-inc.html) — Entanglement First modules (הודגם); [**Quantum Brilliance**](organisation/quantum-brilliance.html) — Quoll / QB-QDK 2.0 (בהפעלה, 6).

*תאים מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה (7).* Diamond-spin quantum computer prototype: L4 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; Diamond-spin quantum computer prototype: L5 `∅G-nvroom` (פער אטלס — light, microwave and RF control of SnV spins; module operated at -271.6 °C (1.55 K)); Entanglement First modules: L4 `∅G-optlink` (פער אטלס — any-to-any optical link used as the connectivity layer, not only as interconnect; basis fo); Entanglement First modules: L10 `∅G-sicolour` (פער אטלס — Si-28 T-centre + silicon-photonics co-fabrication; no foundry node published; fab_diamond ); Quoll / QB-QDK 2.0: L5 `∅G-nvroom` (פער אטלס — room-temperature microwave drive + green-laser initialisation + optical readout; no cryoge); QC2026 Dual Core: L5 `∅G-nvroom` (פער אטלס — room-temperature optical initialisation and microwave control from a standard wall outlet,); SXQ4: L5 `∅G-nvroom` (פער אטלס — optical initialisation and microwave gates at room temperature (18–27 °C), mobile, no cryo).

#### 8.3.16 זוגיות מיורנה — טופולוגי — טטרון (מיורנה) (`topo`)

**The idea.** Four Majorana zero modes on two gate-defined InAs–superconductor wires (`majorana`) hold one qubit at fixed total fermion parity (`enc_parity`, the tetron): Z and X are parities of different mode pairs. Gates would be parity-measurement sequences read by rf quantum capacitance (`g_mbq`, `ro_qcap`). The claim is a topological gap: with the modes far apart, their splitting falls exponentially with wire length and only an arriving quasiparticle flips a parity. Table 8.3's bet, protection in hardware instead of a code, is an existence claim, at the price of Clifford-only gates and no fallback.

**What it needs.** One chain, no alternates: the gate *requires* the wires and the readout, every gate being a measurement; the encoding two wires per tetron; the readout the island's quantum capacitance; the wires an InAs–Pb stack from III-V MBE (`fab_mbe`), whose disorder can mimic Majorana signatures. No *replaces* or *conflicts* edge: with no rival at any layer, one failure strands the architecture. Code, decoder and interconnect are empty (§7.6); a Floquet or measurement-based code awaits a demonstrated qubit. The gate is an empty slot: specified since 2008, never operated.

**Where it stands.** One machine, no device: Majorana 1 (and Majorana 2) (Microsoft), contested, 5/30 in §2.7 [D]. Measured: single-wire parity readout at 1% assignment error, not by itself proof of Majorana modes, the editors noted [D][T1][T2]; tetron Z- and X-loop parity lifetimes of 12.4 ms and 14.5 µs, the X–Z succession test deferred [P][T3]; a ~20 s InAs–Pb parity switching time in one wire of one tetron, called a qubit lifetime by Microsoft's blog [P][T4][C][T5]. No braiding, X measurement on Majorana 2, or two-qubit operation. A Nature Matters Arising disputes the topological reading, answered without concession [D][T8]; QuTech's independent Kitaev-chain parity qubit has limited protection [P][T7].

**Exceptions and borrowings.** None off-slot: the machine fills all seven technologies and fits no other architecture — weak evidence: the architecture was drawn around it; one machine cannot test its width. The code gap (`∅G-pathcode`) is telling: Microsoft's own geometric codes need all-to-all connectivity, which its neutral-atom product offers (§3.3) and the intended tetron lattice does not. Of its own technologies only MBE travels: Belenos and Lucy (Quandela), on the fusion architecture, grow quantum-dot photon sources with it.

**The next test.** Per §5.2, an X-parity lifetime near the Z lifetime, or any two-qubit entanglement, confirms; neither by end-2027 demotes. Since 22 September 2026 DARPA's evaluators have on-site access to a Majorana 2 system in Microsoft's Maryland research centre — access, not a result [C][T10]. Expectation: by March 2028 an X/Z lifetime ratio below 10² (now ~10³ [T3]) or a joint parity measurement across two tetrons, confirmed by DARPA; without either, the tetron is a materials result, not an architecture.

**What the builders say.** Microsoft argues that topological qubits suit error correction through inherently low error rates, small size and digital control [C][T5], and presented Majorana 1 as eight qubits on a chip designed to scale to a million [C][T11]. With lead replacing aluminium in its tetrons (qubits made of two superconducting nanowires with Majorana modes at their ends), it reports in a preprint parity lifetimes near 20 seconds, some reaching a minute [P][T4]. On that basis it halved its timeline and now aims at a scalable, practical quantum computer by 2029, without a qubit count [R][T5]. In September 2026 it gave DARPA full on-site access to its Majorana 2 system for independent testing [C][T10].

**המכונות של ארכיטקטורה זו (במרשם: 2, מהן 0 התקנים, 0 בעלי שערים).** [**Microsoft**](organisation/microsoft.html) — Majorana 1 (אחר), Majorana 2 (אחר).

*תאים מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה (1).* Majorana 1: L7 `∅G-pathcode` (פער אטלס — empty L7 on topo architecture; MS 4D geometric codes [[96,6,8]] need all-to-all, not a tet).

#### 8.3.17 מחשב חישול על קיוביטי שטף — מחשב חישול קוונטי — קיוביטי שטף (`anneal`)

**The idea.** Thousands of rf-SQUID flux qubits (`fluxq`) serve as programmable Ising spins: a loop's persistent current, one way or the other, is the spin; inductive couplers set the interactions; the chip evolves continuously (`g_anneal`) from a transverse-field ground state toward that of the programmed problem. The problem is static: biases are held on chip, the answer latched as bits. Table 8.3's bet, special-purpose scale now, is a trade: count and connectivity for gates, logical qubits and any route to fault tolerance.

**What it needs.** The qubits, the long couplers' multilayer routing (`cx_lr`) and the SFQ flux DACs (`ct_fluxdac`) all *require* one superconducting process (`fab_sc`). Readout (`ro_fluxro`) *requires* the qubits and the DACs: a quantum flux parametron latches each qubit's current, on-chip shift registers carry the bit out. Static biases let DACs control "tens of thousands of qubits and couplers with just 200 bias wires" [C][A8]. The empty layers mean: no encoding, so a qubit is a Hamiltonian term, not a memory; no readout during the anneal, so no syndrome for a code or decoder. Errors are never detected, only averaged over samples: more qubits buy larger problems, not better answers.

**Where it stands.** Three machines, two devices, 14 of 32 cells documented (Table 8.1), no gate. Advantage2 (D-Wave, May 2025; 4,400+ qubits, Zephyr 20-way, 12.5 kW [C][A1]) supersedes Advantage (2020). D-Wave's "beyond-classical" spin-glass result (Science, March 2025) was substantially eroded within weeks by tensor-network and variational methods [D][A2]; D-Wave maintains its largest instances remain unmatched [P][A3]. Qilimanjaro's coherent annealer shows no device, count or benchmark. §2.8 scores only E 4 and F 2; H1-2026 revenue fell 67% [C][A4]; a CHIPS agreement (up to $100 M, 8 September 2026) funds work toward a 100,000-qubit annealer and a gate-model system [C][R][B19].

**Exceptions and borrowings.** No cell falls off the slots; all fit here best. Both Advantage machines list dispersive readout (`ro_disp`), which the map says *replaces* the flux latch, as an alternate: perimeter resonators read the shift-register bits after the latch, so the two sit in series: a hybrid, not rivals. Qilimanjaro's coupler and DAC cells are inferred, its readout undisclosed: a data gap. Borrowings run outward: the D-Wave gate-model dual-rail system takes the flux DACs as an alternate, and IBM Quantum Loon and its successors use the long couplers for qLDPC checks.

**The next test.** §5.2 has no annealing row; D-Wave's roadmap serves. Its 20,000-qubit system for 2029 and 100,000 qubits by 2031 [R][A4] rest on "superconducting interconnect technologies" for "multi-chip fabrics" [C][A4], the layer the architecture leaves empty. Confirmation by March 2028: a multi-chip anneal with couplers across chip boundaries, or a beyond-classical claim surviving a year of classical challenge. Demotion: neither by then, and Advantage3 moved or dropped.

**What the builders say.** D-Wave's Advantage2 has more than 4,400 qubits, each coupled to 20 others so that larger problems can be embedded [C][A9]. D-Wave says an Advantage2 prototype simulated magnetic materials in minutes where a classical supercomputer would need nearly a million years, a result published in Science [D][A10]. It plans a multi-chip Advantage3: 20,000 qubits in 2029 and 100,000 by 2031 [R][A11], wired through a new input-output design needing at most 20% more lines than today's 4,500-qubit machine [C][A11]. Qilimanjaro builds analog processors from fluxonium qubits, which it calls error-resilient by design and usable before fault tolerance; the first, 10 qubits, runs at the Barcelona Supercomputing Center, with two more generations due in 2026 and 2027 [C][A12].

**המכונות של ארכיטקטורה זו (במרשם: 4, מהן 3 התקנים, 0 בעלי שערים).** **D-Wave Quantum** — Advantage3 (מתוכנן, 20,000), Advantage (בהפעלה, 5,000), Advantage2 (בהפעלה, 4,500); [**Qilimanjaro Quantum Tech**](organisation/qilimanjaro-quantum-tech.html) — Qilimanjaro AQPU (Adiabatic Quantum Processing Unit) at BSC (בהפעלה, 10).

*תאים מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה (5).* Advantage3: L4 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; Advantage3: L9 מודולים מרובי-שבבים / מצמדי l (אותו קריוסטט) (`ic_mcm`, ראשית מחוץ למשבצות הארכיטקטורה); Qilimanjaro AQPU (Adiabatic Quantum Processing Unit) at BSC: L4 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; Qilimanjaro AQPU (Adiabatic Quantum Processing Unit) at BSC: L5 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה; Qilimanjaro AQPU (Adiabatic Quantum Processing Unit) at BSC: L6 undisclosed במקום שבו הארכיטקטורה מצפה לטכנולוגיה.

### 8.4 מגמות — שמונה השערות שנבחנו מול המרשם

כל השערה היא טענה הניתנת להפרכה על האוכלוסייה; המבחן הוא החישוב הנקוב לצידה, שתהליך הבנייה מריץ על השורות הנוכחיות של המרשם לפי כלל המודפס יחד עם המבחן; הפסק הוא *נתמכת*, *נתמכת חלקית* או *אינה נתמכת*, יחד עם גודל המדגם. על מה נשען כל פסק, ומה היה הופך אותו, מפורט ב-§8.7. הניסוח והכלל של השערה מוקפאים כאשר המהדורה מקבלת חותמת שחרור; טענה ששונתה מקבלת מספר חדש, והישנה מוצאת משימוש ואינה מנוסחת מחדש לעולם — כך שהיסטוריית הפסקים בסוף סעיף זה, שורה אחת לכל מהדורה ולצידה גודל המרשם, מראה כיצד פסק נסחף ככל שהמרשם גדל.

**H1 — מספר הקיוביטים הפיזיים עבר אל האטומים; מחשבים קוונטיים של מעגלים מוליכי-על כבר אינם מתחרים במספר.**

*מבחן.* חציון הקיוביטים הפיזיים לכל משפחה, על פני כל ההתקנים ועל פני ההתקנים בעלי השערים (100 מתוך 126; בלי סימולטורים אנלוגיים, מערכים ללא שער שזירה ומערכי ניסוי של קיוביט יחיד); ההתקן בעל השערים הגדול ביותר בכל משפחה; חציוני קוהורטים לשתי המשפחות הגדולות ביותר.

| משפחה | התקנים: חציון קיוביטים (n) | בעלי שערים: חציון קיוביטים (n) | ההתקן בעל השערים הגדול ביותר | ההתקן הגדול ביותר מכל סוג |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| אטומים ניטרליים | 256 (11) | 140 (7) | Phoenix (Gen-1) and Gen-2 — 1,180 | 6,100-qubit tweezer array — 6,100 |
| מעגלים מוליכי-על | 53 (67) | 69 (58) | IBM Quantum Condor — 1,121 | IBM Quantum Condor — 1,121 |
| יונים לכודים | 30 (17) | 25 (16) | Helios — 98 | 2-D site-resolved ion simulator — 512 |
| פוטוניקה | 12 (4) | 12 (3) | Lucy — 12 | Lucy — 12 |
| ספינים במוליכים למחצה | 11.5 (6) | 11.5 (6) | HRL exchange-only silicon spin processor (18 EO qubits from 54 quantum dots) — 18 | HRL exchange-only silicon spin processor (18 EO qubits from 54 quantum dots) — 18 |

*תוצאה.* האטומים מובילים ביחס של ×4.8 בכל ההתקנים וביחס של ×2.0 בהתקנים בעלי השערים. חציוני הקוהורטים (בעלי שערים; n בסוגריים) — מעגלים מוליכי-על: 2021 40 (6), 2022 52.5 (4), 2023 108.5 (6), 2024 105 (13), 2025 50 (19), 2026 48 (10); אטומים ניטרליים: 2023 1,180 (1), 2024 140 (1), 2025 180 (4), 2026 20 (1). *פסק:* **נתמכת**. היתרון של האטומים במספר הקיוביטים אמיתי בקרב המעבדים, וגדול בהרבה בקרב מערכי המלכודות: המכונות של אלפי קיוביטים הן מערכים שעדיין אינם מבצעים שער שזירה, ואילו מעבד הפינצטות בעל השערים הגדול ביותר וסריג הטרנסמונים הגדול ביותר רחוקים זה מזה פי כמה בלבד. חציוני הקוהורטים זזים יותר לפי מי שנכנס למרשם בשנה נתונה (שבבים ראשונים וקטנים של שחקנים חדשים) מאשר לפי המובילים, והם דלים מדי בכל קוהורט כדי לשאת מגמה בכוחות עצמם.

**H2 — שגיאת השער הדו-קיוביטי מתכנסת בחציון בין שלוש המשפחות הגדולות, ואילו המספר הבודד הטוב ביותר נשאר אצל היונים.**

*מבחן.* החציון והערך הטוב ביותר של `err_2q_median` על פני ההתקנים בכל משפחה, בלי ערכי זוגות שיא, מטרות והדגמות של רכיבים. *תוצאה.* מעגלים מוליכי-על: חציון 4.2×10⁻³, הטוב ביותר 1.0×10⁻³ (Aqumen Seeker), n = 24; יונים לכודים: חציון 4.0×10⁻³, הטוב ביותר 7.9×10⁻⁴ (Helios), n = 7; אטומים ניטרליים: חציון 4.0×10⁻³, הטוב ביותר 1.6×10⁻³ (448-atom fault-tolerant processor), n = 5; פוטוניקה: חציון 6.2×10⁻², הטוב ביותר 6.2×10⁻² (Belenos), n = 2; ספינים במוליכים למחצה: חציון 5.3×10⁻³, הטוב ביותר 3.0×10⁻³ (HRL exchange-only silicon spin processor (18 EO qubits from 54 quantum dots)), n = 4. הפיזור בין שלושת החציונים הגדולים הוא ×1.1. *פסק:* **נתמכת**. התכנסות של החציונים לצד פער מתמשך בערכים הטובים ביותר היא מה שמצופה כאשר את החציון קובעות המכונות הרבות מהשורה השנייה, ואת הערך הטוב ביותר — מעבדות ספורות שמפעילות את אותה פלטפורמה כבר עשור. הפסק מתאר תמונת מצב אחת של המרשם; השאלה אם החציונים ימשיכו להתכנס שייכת לקוהורטים של המהדורה הבאה, לא לזו.
